   145	\subsection{The per-step cost is not constant in concurrency}
   146	
   147	Equation~\ref{eq:busy} would be uninteresting if $\tau$ were a constant. It is
   148	not. Across our 13 runs $\tau_1$ ranges from 27.0 to 36.7\,ms and $\tau_2$
   149	from 20.6 to 33.1\,ms, and both rise with the running count: fitting
   150	Eq.~\ref{eq:elasticity} to the five device-only configurations gives
   151	$\varepsilon = 0.38$ for $\tau_1$ and $0.61$ for $\tau_2$
   152	($R^2 = 0.89$ for the latter, Fig.~\ref{fig:cancel}).
   153	
   154	The relation is a trend, not a law. Two host-tier configurations with similar
   155	running counts behave differently: \texttt{cap4\_hc} runs 1.73 requests at
   156	29.8\,ms while \texttt{F:hz0} runs 1.66 at 35.7\,ms. Running count sets a
   157	floor and a trend, not a value---and on the host-tier family, as we show below,
   158	it does not set the value at all.
   159	
   160	This is the crux of the paper, and we state it at the strength the arithmetic
   161	supports. Hold $\tau_1$ at its value for \texttt{cap1}, 28.8\,ms, and busy
   162	time falls as $1/\overline{R}$: raising the running count from 1.00 to 1.84
   163	should have cut busy time by a factor of 1.84, from 340 minutes to 184. The
   164	measured curve flattens and lands at 234. The rise in $\tau$ takes back 50 of
   165	the 156 minutes that the extra concurrency would otherwise have bought---\emph{a
   166	third} of the gain, not all of it and, as an earlier revision of this paper
   167	claimed, not more than half. The other two thirds are real: raising
   168	concurrency does make the engine faster. What it does not do is make it faster
   169	in proportion, and the shortfall compounds with every controller that tries to
   170	exploit the difference.
   171	
   172	\paragraph{What the engine is doing when it is not decoding.} SGLang exposes
   173	two counters that answer this directly: tokens evicted from the radix cache,
   174	and prompt tokens that had to be prefilled rather than reused. Across the five
   175	static caps \emph{without} HiCache the two track each other almost exactly.
   176	\texttt{cap1} evicts 9.2\,M tokens and prefills 8.6\,M uncached;
   177	\texttt{cap5} evicts 21.3\,M and prefills 20.7\,M; the gap is 0.6--0.7\,M on
   178	every one of the five. An evicted prefix is recomputed, close to one token for
   179	one, and that recomputation is chunked into decode steps. Those same five runs
   180	give recomputation rising with $\tau$ across four of the five points: 9.2\,M
   181	uncached tokens at 27.4\,ms, 11.5 at 29.7, 15.4 at 32.4, 20.7 at 36.7. The
   182	exception is the first point, \texttt{cap1}, which recomputes least (8.6\,M)
   183	and still costs 28.8\,ms---more than \texttt{cap2} does for more recomputation.
   184	We report the ordering we have rather than rounding it to a monotone one: it is
   185	a mechanism consistent with the $\tau$ trend on four of the five device-only
   186	configurations, and one point it does not order. It is five points on five
   187	different policies, not an
   188	intervention: we did not hold concurrency fixed and vary eviction, so we call
   189	it consistent rather than demonstrated.
   190	
   191	\begin{figure}[tb]
   192	  \centering
   193	  \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figs/fig7_elasticity.pdf}
   194	  \caption{Per-step cost against the running count during busy time. Solid
   195	  circles are $\tau_1$ and squares $\tau_2$ for the five device-only
   196	  configurations; $\tau_1$ is model-consistent (it averages over all engine
   197	  steps, including prefill that emits no output token), $\tau_2$ is the
   198	  per-request decode interval, and $\tau_1$ exceeds $\tau_2$ by 8--40\%. The
   199	  green triangles are $\tau_1$ for the three HiCache configurations---the ones
   200	  the controllers run---and they are drawn against the same axes to make
   201	  \S\ref{sec:criterion}'s point visible: over $\overline{R}_{\text{busy}}$ 1.25
   202	  to 1.73, which covers the whole controller operating range, the host-tier
   203	  curve rises 27.0 to 29.8\,ms while the device-only curve over a comparable
   204	  span rises 27.4 to 36.7. We plot $\tau_1$ only for the HiCache points to keep
   205	  the figure readable. The fitted exponent is the elasticity $\varepsilon$ of
   206	  Eq.~\ref{eq:elasticity}; it is fit to the device-only points alone.}
   207	  \label{fig:cancel}
   208	\end{figure}
   209	
   210	\paragraph{Where the mechanism stops.} With HiCache enabled the
   211	correspondence breaks---and it breaks in a way that confirms the mechanism
   212	rather than undermining it. The device cache misses as much as before:
   213	\texttt{cap4\_hc} misses 22.4\,M tokens on the device, more than the
   214	device-only \texttt{cap4} misses. But only 9.1\,M of that is recomputed; the
   215	other 13.3\,M is served from the host tier. Across the ten host-tier runs
   216	the host serves 12--19\,M tokens each, and across the five device-only runs it
   217	serves \emph{exactly zero}---the device miss equals the recomputed count to
   218	within rounding on all five. Recomputation is still what the device cannot
   219	absorb; the host tier is a second absorber in front of it.
   220	
   320	device pool, so three times in total), then shrinking the device pool alone
   321	should move the collapse point even with no host tier involved. We do not
   322	treat the arithmetic as a result---it assumes KV demand grows linearly in the
   323	running count, which heterogeneous sessions need not satisfy---and the
   324	queued experiment measures the collapse point directly rather than predicting
   325	it, and \S\ref{sec:results} reports it once it has run.
   326	
   327	\paragraph{What is left unexplained.} Not everything here is accounted for.
   328	The host-tier runs sit at a narrow band of recomputed tokens (8.6--10.4\,M)
   329	\emph{and} of realization (84.2--92.4\%), yet $\tau_1$ spans 27.0--36.2\,ms
   330	across them.
   331	Part of that is now explained and part is not. Four of those runs
   332	(\texttt{F:hz2} 33.6, \texttt{F:bres} 34.1, \texttt{F:hz0} 35.7,
   333	\texttt{F:breshz1} 36.2\,ms) were
   334	measured while three other engine instances shared the host, which inflates
   335	per-step cost by 25--40\% and leaves the volume columns untouched
   336	(\S\ref{sec:repro}); they are being re-measured and we do not use their
   337	timing. Excluding them the remaining six span 27.0--31.5\,ms at flat
   338	recomputation. That residual is small but it is not zero, and we do not
   339	attribute it. We note that the negative result does not rest on it: the
   340	cancellation is measured directly, not inferred from the mechanism.
   341	
   342	One caution about the strength of the account above, since the arithmetic
   343	invites overreading. $\text{reuse} + \text{recomputed} = \text{prompt}$ is a
   344	definition, not a finding, so ``the shortfall reappears as recomputation''
   345	follows from how we partitioned the prompt and cannot by itself establish the
   346	mechanism. What is independent of the partition is the pair of engine
   347	counters: evicted tokens and uncached prefill tokens are separate SGLang
   348	counters, and on the device-only runs they track within 0.6--0.7\,M on all
   349	five---which is the observation that licenses reading eviction as the cause
   350	of recomputation. With a host tier the two come apart by design, and the
   351	13--19\,M gap between them is the host's contribution.
   352	
   353	\paragraph{A refutation we had to withdraw.} An earlier revision withdrew this
   354	mechanism outright, on two checks that were both wrong. The first was that
   355	total prefilled tokens equalled the trace's planned prefill to a ratio of 1.00,
    20	earlier harness revision, and we never ran the all-throttles-off arm on the
    21	25-session tier under the current one. So the reversal is a direction we
    22	measured across two harness generations, not a same-generation paired
    23	comparison, and a reviewer should read it that way. The same-generation
    24	25-session measurement we do have is in Table~\ref{tab:tier25}, and it does not
    25	contain that arm.
    26	
    27	This is the regime in which adaptive control should win, and we now have the
    28	same-generation measurement that earlier drafts of this paper were missing.
    29	Four arms on the 25-session tier, all under the current harness
    30	(Table~\ref{tab:tier25}): static caps of two, three and four finish in 32.4,
    31	24.2 and 23.6 minutes at 92.8\%, 91.2\% and 79.2\% SLO, and \sys{} finishes
    32	in 27.6 minutes at 92.8\%.
    33	
    34	\begin{table}[tb]
    35	  \caption{The 25-session tier, all four arms under the current harness.
    36	  This is the same-generation comparison earlier drafts of this paper were
    37	  missing; \texttt{cap4} and the \sys{} arm were run for this revision.}
    38	  \label{tab:tier25}
    39	  \centering
    40	  \small
    41	  \setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
    42	  \begin{tabular}{lrrrr}
    43	    \toprule
    44	    Policy & Wall (min) & Conc. & SLO (\%) & Seg.\ SD (pp) \\
    45	    \midrule
    46	    cap2          & 32.4 & 1.95 & 92.8 & 11.0 \\
    47	    cap3          & 24.2 & 2.77 & 91.2 &  5.1 \\
    48	    cap4          & 23.6 & 3.39 & 79.2 & 21.4 \\
    49	    \sys{}        & 27.6 & 2.47 & 92.8 &  7.1 \\
    50	    \bottomrule
    51	  \end{tabular}
    52	\end{table}
    53	
    54	The finding is a trade, not a defeat, and it is the opposite of what the
    55	200-session tier shows. Against the same-generation envelope---which runs from
    56	\texttt{cap4} at (23.6, 79.2) through \texttt{cap3} at (24.2, 91.2) to
    57	\texttt{cap2} at (32.4, 92.8)---\sys{} sits $+0.9$\,pp above the line. A
    58	static cap of three is still 12\% faster in wall clock, but it gives up
    59	1.6\,pp of SLO to do it, so neither dominates the other. On this tier the
    60	controller is a different point on the front rather than a dominated one,
    61	which is exactly what the 200-session tier denies it.
    62	
    63	We are careful about the size, and about the yardstick. $+0.9$\,pp is
    64	measured against a straight-line interpolation between static points, not
    65	against a switching policy anyone ran, so the defensible statement is that
    66	\sys{} is not dominated by any static point we measured on this tier---not
    67	that it beats what a static mix could achieve. On 125 requests one request is
    68	worth 0.8\,pp, so the margin is about one request. The tier's
    69	segment-to-segment SD (5.1 to 21.4\,pp) is not the right yardstick: segment
    70	volatility is not run-to-run uncertainty. The right one is the replicate spread
    71	we have---which, across the five configurations we repeated, is 0.5, 0.8, 1.0,
    72	3.9 and 5.1\,pp (\texttt{replicates.csv}). Against the two tightest of those $+0.9$\,pp is about a replicate
    73	wide; against the three widest---which belong to the configurations that decide
    74	our headline elsewhere---it is inside one. We quote the tightest pair
    75	because it is the least favourable to us, not because it is the whole
    76	distribution. What we will defend is the weaker claim: on the
    77	low-load tier the controller is not worse, and the same switch that is worth
    78	$+51\%$ there costs $-42\%$ at high load. A controller that is competitive
    79	where load is light and dominated where load is heavy is the pattern this
    80	paper set out to explain, and it is the pattern we measured.

    20	controllers' failure, on their own configuration and on a second workload, it

    22	whether a controller will win.
    23	
    24	\subsection{What we measured}
    25	
    26	We serve a replay of 200 real coding-agent sessions on a single RTX 3090
    27	(Qwen2.5-Coder-7B, 96K context, SGLang, a 101{,}432-token KV pool) and compare
    28	a family of admission policies against a sweep of static concurrency caps.
    29	Nine controller variants, spanning four mechanistically distinct ideas, all
    30	land below the static Pareto front, and so does a reimplementation of an
    31	independent congestion-based controller from the literature
    32	(Fig.~\ref{fig:frontier}). The best of them misses by under a point---inside the
    33	$\pm1.0$\,pp band we call a tie, but on the below side of it---and its repeat
    34	misses by 5.6.
    35	
    36	\begin{figure}[tb]
    37	  \centering
    38	  \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figs/fig1_frontier.pdf}
    39	  \caption{Every completed run on the (wall clock, SLO) plane. The grey line
    40	  is the upper-left envelope of the static points---drawn from the data, so it
    41	  uses each configuration's \emph{better} run where a repeat exists, which is
    42	  why \texttt{cap3}+HiCache's 236-minute run sits below the line rather than on
    43	  it; a policy below or on it has
    44	  not beaten a static cap, because time-slicing two caps approximates any
    45	  point on the segment between them. Nine \sys{} variants plus a
   145	        knob rather than a hypothesis, and we report it only because it slides
   146	        the operating point along the front and adds no mechanism. The fourth,
   147	        changing the execution granularity from session count to
   148	        running-request count, is falsified by measurement \emph{and} by an
   149	        argument about why the permit mechanism cannot express the control it
   150	        would need. We do not call the last two of these falsifications of a
   151	        stated hypothesis, because they were not.
   152	  \item We give a two-part decomposition of the wall clock (\S\ref{sec:model})
   153	        that locates the cancellation: admission policy moves the
   154	        running-request count, and the per-step cost moves with it. On the
   155	        device-only configurations we can account for that rise token for
   156	        token---evicted prefixes are recomputed almost one for one. On
   157	        host-tier configurations recomputation is flat and the account has to
   158	        come from elsewhere; we report the part of that residual we were able
   159	        to attribute, and the part we were not (\S\ref{sec:model},
   160	        \S\ref{sec:repro}).
   161	  \item We reduce the question to one measurable number, the elasticity of
   162	        per-step cost with respect to the running count (\S\ref{sec:criterion}),
   163	        and measure it at $0.38$ on the model-consistent step cost and $0.61$
   164	        on the per-request one, on the configurations whose mechanism we can
   165	        account for token for token. We offer it as a diagnostic rather than a
   166	        predictor: it says where on the curve a policy operates and how much
   167	        the remaining headroom is worth, and \S\ref{sec:criterion} is explicit
   168	        about the sense in which it is circular and the sense in which it is
   169	        not.
   170	  \item We characterize when adaptivity \emph{could} have paid, and show that
   171	        our workload is not that case: the same throttle configuration that is
   172	        worth $+51\%$ at 25 sessions costs $-42\%$ at 200 (\S\ref{sec:load}).
   173	        Put differently, no single scalar cap is optimal across both, which is
   174	        the regime adaptive control exists for. On the low-load tier, where
   175	        that argument bites hardest, the controller is \emph{not} worse---%
   176	        same-generation measurements put it on the front, $+0.9$\,pp above the
   177	        line and inside the noise. On the high-load tier it is dominated. That
   178	        asymmetry, not a blanket failure, is what this paper measures.
   179	  \item We make the measurements reproducible: the front and each controller's
   180	        signed distance are produced by one script from the raw per-step logs,
   181	        the tables and the sub-thousand-step runs by two others, all named in
   182	        \S\ref{sec:repro}. We report the six claims we had to retract along the
   183	        way, with the checks that caught them.
   184	\end{itemize}
   185	
   100	  and say why. \S\ref{sec:repro} gives the audit, the threshold and the
   101	  evidence.}

   475	gate-of-four arm completed (999 steps); the gate-of-two arm reached 770 and is
   476	not used, so what we report below is a single comparison against the no-gate
   477	arm rather than a sweep.
   478	
   479	It does not work, and the measurement says why. Against the no-gate arm, a
   480	gate of four moved mean running requests from 1.33 to 1.30 and mean live
   481	sessions from 3.25 to 3.03: it tightened both, by 2\% and 7\%, rather than
   482	pinning the one it aims at. That is what we
   483	observe, and we state it as the observation it is rather than deriving a bound
   484	we cannot support. A live session spends most of its time parked in a tool wait
   485	(the ratio of mean running requests to mean live sessions is 0.38 to 0.66
   486	across our arms), so pausing live sessions removes capacity that was not
   487	running in the first place---but that ratio is an average over a run, not a
   488	bound on any instant, and the running count can in principle reach the live
   489	count. Both repairs fail: without a
   490	constraint on admission, a paused session is re-admitted on the next cycle and
   491	blocks for at most one 2\,s cycle; with one, the policy is a session cap again.
   492	The obstacle is granularity---\texttt{paused} takes effect at round
   493	boundaries, while pinning an instantaneous count requires control the
   494	permit mechanism does not have.
   495	
   496	The measured outcome matches: the gate moved running concurrency from 1.33 to
   497	1.30 and live sessions from 3.25 to 3.03. It tightened rather than pinned, and
   498	a tightening is another cap.
   499	
   500	\begin{figure}[tb]
   501	  \centering
   502	  \includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figs/fig6_horizon.pdf}
   503	  \caption{The growth reserve is not a free parameter. Removing it costs
   504	  12.6\,pp of SLO; two rounds is 15 minutes slower than three. The optimum at
   505	  one round comes closest to the static front, 0.9\,pp below it---and is the
   506	  one point here that does not reproduce on a repeat (it lands 5.6\,pp
   507	  below).
   508	  The other three settings were each run once, so the shape is better
   509	  supported than any single point on it.}
   510	  \label{fig:horizon}
   145	limitation these works would press on.
   146	
   147	\paragraph{Prefix caching.} The prefix cache is often proposed as the signal
   148	an admission policy should use. What we measure is that the \emph{realized}
   149	reuse is a function of the admission policy and of the host tier, while the
   150	\emph{planned} reuse is a constant of the trace; an earlier draft of this
   151	paper conflated the two and got the direction wrong in both. The trace plans
   152	a round-1-and-later hit rate of 80.9\%. Without HiCache the realized
   153	round-1+ rate follows the plan only at the loosest cap and then collapses:
   154	80.8\%, 78.5\%, 70.6\%, 56.0\%, 32.8\% for caps of one through five. With
   155	HiCache it tracks the plan at every cap we ran: 80.7\%, 79.6\%, 78.7\% for
   156	caps of two, three and four. Our controller sits at 80.6\%.
   157	So the honest statement is not that admission does not move this quantity---%
   158	\texttt{cap4} alone moves it 25 points---but that the host tier is what keeps
   159	it from moving, which is the same mechanism \S\ref{sec:model} and
   160	\S\ref{sec:criterion} keep running into. A configuration without a host tier
   161	that admits four sessions does not have the cache that a host-tier one at the
   162	same concurrency does. All eight numbers in this paragraph are the
   163	\emph{observed} per-step hit rate (\texttt{server\_prefix\_hit\_rate} in the
   164	per-step logs), unweighted mean over rounds $\ge 1$; the planned rate is the
   165	trace constant 80.9\% and does not move with the cap. \S\ref{sec:model} uses
   166	a different convention---all steps, token-weighted, so that reuse and
   167	recomputation partition the prompt---under which the same collapse reads
   168	92.6\%$\to$36.4\% of the trace's bound for the device-only caps and
   169	92.3\%$\to$90.2\% with a host tier. We state both because the paper's earlier
   170	drafts mixed them. We note the boundary of this claim---we varied admission policy,
   171	not eviction policy, and SGLang exposes alternate eviction policies we did not
   172	test. A characterization of production KV-cache traffic at a large cloud
   173	provider finds that eviction-policy design is ``highly workload-dependent''
   174	and that reuse patterns differ sharply by request category
   175	\cite{wang2025kvcachewild}; that is reason to expect a single trace family to

     1	\section{Decomposing the Wall Clock}
     2	\label{sec:model}
     3	
     4	\subsection{Two components}
     5	
     6	We split the wall clock at every sampled instant according to whether the
     7	engine has any request in flight:
     8	\begin{equation}
     9	  W \;=\; W_{\text{busy}} \;+\; W_{\text{idle}} .
    10	  \label{eq:split}
    11	\end{equation}
    12	Fig.~\ref{fig:decomp} shows the split for every completed run. Two things are
    13	immediately visible and both matter later.
    14	
    15	First, idle time is not noise. Across the runs it spans 7.4\% to 41.9\% of
    16	the sampled span---7.4\% for the fastest configuration, 36.3\% for the
    17	slowest, and 41.9\% for the one that idles most (all-throttles-off). The
    18	denominator here
    19	is the span the sampler observed, which runs about six minutes longer than the
    20	request wall clock of Table~\ref{tab:runs}, so the fractions against that
    21	wall clock are 7.6\% and 36.7\%---and it is not spread evenly.
    22	Over 1{,}849 stretches the mean is 20.6\,s and 17.6\% of them exceed
    23	20\,s, accounting for 69.6\% of all idle \emph{time}
    24	(Fig.~\ref{fig:idle}). The tail, not the middle, is what a scheduler feels,
    25	and it sits at the scale of the 15.8--16.9\,s mean tool wait (demanded and
    26	measured; \texttt{toolwait.csv}). We report the mean and the share rather
    27	than the median here because the median is not robust to which runs enter the
    28	pool: it is 10.0\,s without \texttt{cap1} and 5.0--5.6\,s with it, since
    29	that one run contributes 411 of the 1{,}849 stretches and is itself the
    30	longest. The mean (18.3--21.0\,s) and the over-20\,s share of idle time
    31	(64.8--70.6\%) move by little across the four poolings we report in
    32	\texttt{idle\_pooled.csv}, so the claim that the tail dominates does not
    33	depend on the choice. The 90th percentile moves with the median
    34	(25.1--30.1\,s) and we give it as a range for the same reason.
    35	
    36	Second, the two components move independently. \texttt{bres}
    37	(\S\ref{sec:results}) reduces idle from 15.9\% to 10.1\%, recovering about 17
    38	minutes---and is nonetheless \emph{slower} than its own baseline, because its

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""九条控制器臂的 **busy 条件** 在跑数均值 —— 论文 §IV 与归因表必须用同一个口径。
     3	
     4	为什么需要：§IV 原来写「九条臂跑在 1.35~1.69」，而那个列表是**硬编码**在
     5	gen_elasticity.py 里、注释还指错了文件（说「见 occupancy.csv」，但
     6	occupancy.csv 的 running_mean 是全时段均值，范围 0.97~1.41，根本不含 1.63+）。
     7	同一篇稿子两页之后的归因表又用了全时段均值当 R̄ 列。同一个符号两个口径。
     8	
     9	busy 条件均值 = 全时段均值 / busy 占比，与 gen_decomp.py 里 τ₁ 的分母一致。
    10	
    11	用法: python3 gen_arm_running.py <results_dir> <out.csv>
    12	"""
    13	import csv, os, statistics, sys
    14	
    15	# sf0_r2 与 hc_r2 必须在里面：归因表引用 sf0 的在跑数，而它原来不在本表里，
    16	# gen_attribution 于是静默回退到 occupancy.csv 的【全时段均值】口径（1.26），
    17	# 而其余 11 行都是 busy 条件口径（sf0 的 busy 值是 1.45）—— 口径混用（2026-09-24 审稿人抓）。
    18	# 这就是 CLAUDE.md 纪律 3 的又一个实例：生成器的 run 列表不全会静默改变下游口径。
    19	ARMS = ["foyer_sf0_r2", "foyer_hc", "foyer_hc_r2", "foyer_hz1", "foyer_hz2",
    20	        "foyer_hz0", "foyer_bres", "foyer_breshz1", "foyer_nogate", "foyer_rg4",
    21	        "foyfix_hc", "aimd2", "aimd2_paper"]
    22	STATIC = ["cap1", "cap2", "cap3_fixed", "cap4", "cap5",
    23	          "pairB_cap2hc", "cap3_hc", "cap4_hc", "cap4_hc_r2"]
    24	
    25	
    26	def one(path):
    27	    rd = list(csv.DictReader(open(path)))
    28	    rn = [float(x["sglang:num_running_reqs"]) for x in rd if x["sglang:num_running_reqs"]]
    29	    if not rn:
    30	        return None
    31	    allm = statistics.mean(rn)
    32	    busy_frac = sum(1 for v in rn if v > 0) / len(rn)
    33	    return allm, (allm / busy_frac if busy_frac else 0.0), busy_frac
    34	
    35	
    36	def main():
    37	    root, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    38	    rows = []
    39	    print("%-16s %8s %9s %8s" % ("run", "R̄全时", "R̄busy", "busy占比"))
    40	    for group, names in (("controller", ARMS), ("static", STATIC)):
    41	        for r in names:
    42	            p = os.path.join(root, "pois200_" + r, "metrics.csv")
    43	            if not os.path.exists(p):
    44	                continue
    45	            res = one(p)
    46	            if not res:
    47	                continue
    48	            a, b, f = res
    49	            rows.append({"run": r, "family": group, "running_all": round(a, 3),
    50	                         "running_busy": round(b, 3), "busy_frac": round(f, 3)})
    51	            print("%-16s %8.2f %9.2f %8.3f" % (r, a, b, f))
    52	    with open(out, "w", newline="") as f:
    53	        f.write("# 在跑数的两个口径 —— 由 scripts/lab/gen_arm_running.py 生成\n")
    54	        f.write("# running_all: metrics.csv 的 sglang:num_running_reqs 全时段均值\n")
    55	        f.write("# running_busy: running_all / busy占比 —— 论文 §III 与 §IV 一律用这个\n")
    56	        f.write("# 论文里引用 R̄ 时必须指明是哪一个；两个口径差 1.3~1.7 倍\n")
    57	        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
    58	        w.writeheader(); w.writerows(rows)
    59	    ctrl = [r["running_busy"] for r in rows if r["family"] == "controller"]
    60	    if ctrl:
    61	        print()
    62	        print("控制器臂 R̄busy: %.2f ~ %.2f（%d 条）" % (min(ctrl), max(ctrl), len(ctrl)))
    63	        print("  >=1.53 的: %d 条" % sum(1 for x in ctrl if x >= 1.53))
    64	    print("->", out)
    65	
    66	
    67	if __name__ == "__main__":
    68	    main()

pre={x['run'].replace('pois200_',''):x for x in C('paper/data/runs_precise.csv')};runs={x['run']:x for x in C('paper/data/runs.csv') if x['tier']=='200'}
tab=C('paper/data/table1.csv');aa=['cap4_hc_r2','cap3_hc_r2','pairB_cap2hc','cap1'];a=[(float(pre[n]['wall_min']),float(pre[n]['slo_pct'])) for n in aa]
def front(w):
 for (w0,s0),(w1,s1) in zip(a,a[1:]):
  if w0-1e-6<=w<=w1+1e-6:return s0+(s1-s0)*(w-w0)/(w1-w0)
 return None
bad=[];precise_missing=[];raw_missing=[];ranges=[]
for r in tab:
 n=r['run'];p='results/night/pois200_'+n+'/steps.jsonl'
 if os.path.exists(p):
  st=[json.loads(x) for x in open(p) if x.strip()];sub=[x['submit_timestamp'] for x in st if x.get('submit_timestamp')];comp=[x['complete_timestamp'] for x in st if x.get('complete_timestamp')];tt=[x['first_token_ms'] for x in st if x.get('first_token_ms') is not None]
  w=(max(comp)-min(sub))/60;s=100*sum(v<=10000 for v in tt)/len(tt)
  src='raw'
 else:
  raw_missing.append(n)
  if n in pre:w=float(pre[n]['wall_min']);s=float(pre[n]['slo_pct']);src='precise'
  else:w=float(runs[n]['wall_min']);s=float(runs[n]['slo_pct']);src='rounded';precise_missing.append(n)
 f=front(w);d=s-f if f is not None else None
 err=None if d is None else d-float(r['delta_pp'])
 if d is None or abs(err)>.0003 or abs(w-float(r['wall_min']))>.0003 or abs(s-float(r['slo_pct']))>.0003:bad.append((n,src,round(w,6),round(s,6),round(d,5) if d else d,r['delta_pp'],round(err,5) if err else err))
 if d is not None and r['anchor']=='0' and 'foyer' in n:ranges.append((abs(abs(d)-1),n,round(d,5)))
print('anchors',a)
print('rows',len(tab),'raw_missing',raw_missing,'precise_missing',precise_missing)
print('mismatches',bad)
print('closest_controller_band',sorted(ranges)[:8])

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'

三处重点：当前稿中的 **36.3% 只指 cap1 空转份额**；设备侧兑现下限已写为 36.4%，两者不应互换。[空转](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:16) · [兑现表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:244) 同一 cap1 的 **92.6%（稿中写法）是“占 trace 上界”**，**69.9% 是“占 prompt”**；[模型节第 264–268 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:264)已明确区分，但 92.6 本身舍入有误。当前正文的 **0.55** 三处均指同一 single-flight-off 前沿差，没有第二种含义。[结果节](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:13) · [表 I](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:126)

真正需要改写的一处是“**too low by exactly the busy fraction—36.3%**”：36.3% 是 *idle* fraction。使用全时段均值会把估计乘以 busy fraction（约 63.7%），即低估约 36.3%。[原句](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:91) · [原始分解](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/decomp.csv:6)

Table I 另有 **5 行原始步骤日志在本地缺失**；其中 4 行可用 [`runs_precise.csv`](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/runs_precise.csv:1)核对，`default` 只有较粗的表输入。因此“其余 23 行一致”包含这项来源限制，不能称作 26 行全部独立原始日志复核。

## (3) 数值移动是否改变定性主张

**没有发现因这次数值移动而翻转的排序或判决。** 即便沿用 36.3% 下限、78.9% 中点及旧斜率，device-only 仍随并发明显下跌，host-tier 的 90.2–92.4% 静态范围仍与之分离；两条 trace 的斜率形状判断也不变。[模型表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:243) · [跨 trace 判断](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_xtrace.tex:35)

用旧的一位小数前沿锚点与现有全精度锚点逐行比较，**Table I 无判决换档**；旧锚点使最接近边界的 horizon=1 约为 −0.944 pp，现为 −0.928 pp，均在 ±1 pp 平局带内。[锚点](/root/paper/foyer/scripts/lab/frontier.py:25) · [判决规则](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/table1.csv:4) 该点离 −1 pp 门槛仅约 **0.072 pp**，所以它本身较敏感；但**这次舍入修正**没有制造或消除平局。正文的“无人超过前沿”也不依赖上述几百分之一个百分点。[结果判词](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:10)

## Acceptance assessment

以目前稿件、仅凭本次三项数值审查，给 **约 20–35%** 的 CCF-B／CAS Q2 接收机会判断。定性负结果未靠舍入翻盘，是有利证据；但摘要级的 92.6% 错舍入、三条宣称使用全精度却实际用了三位均值的斜率，以及 Table I 三个回退到粗输入的 Δ，会削弱审稿人对整套测量流水线的信任。这个范围是审稿判断，不是打分或统计预测。

三处重点：当前稿中的 **36.3% 只指 cap1 空转份额**；设备侧兑现下限已写为 36.4%，两者不应互换。[空转](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:16) · [兑现表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:244) 同一 cap1 的 **92.6%（稿中写法）是“占 trace 上界”**，**69.9% 是“占 prompt”**；[模型节第 264–268 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:264)已明确区分，但 92.6 本身舍入有误。当前正文的 **0.55** 三处均指同一 single-flight-off 前沿差，没有第二种含义。[结果节](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:13) · [表 I](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:126)

真正需要改写的一处是“**too low by exactly the busy fraction—36.3%**”：36.3% 是 *idle* fraction。使用全时段均值会把估计乘以 busy fraction（约 63.7%），即低估约 36.3%。[原句](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:91) · [原始分解](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/decomp.csv:6)

## (2) 原始来源重算

| 检查 | 结论 |
|---|---|
| 主 trace 上界与 `reuse.csv` 全部 **15 行**的 prompt、uncached、reuse 及兑现比例 | **MATCH**。上界为 21,540,371 / 28,526,871；cap1 为 19,934,847 / 21,540,371 = **92.5464% → 92.5%**，占 prompt 为 **69.9%**。[原始步骤](/root/paper/foyer/results/night/pois200_cap1/steps.jsonl:1) · [汇总](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/reuse.csv:19) |
| 第二条 trace 上界与 `reuse_x.csv` 全部 **5 行** | **MATCH**；上界 6,253,400 / 7,248,629，兑现率为 74.8、72.2、69.4、67.5、64.0%。[数据](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/reuse_x.csv:12) |
| 正文兑现百分比 | **MISMATCH**：所有把最佳主 trace 兑现率写成 **92.6%** 的地方均应为 **92.5%**，包括摘要、引言、模型表及讨论。[摘要](/root/paper/foyer/paper/main.tex:49) · [模型表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:244) · [讨论](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_discussion.tex:168) 此外“host tier 92.3%→90.2%”的起点按所列数据应为 **92.4%**。[引言](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_intro.tex:80) · [讨论](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_discussion.tex:169) |
| `slope.csv` 全部 **9 条**，以原始 `metrics.csv` 的未舍入忙时均值和步骤 token 数重算 | **MISMATCH 3 条**：cap1→cap5 **−66.77**（CSV −66.73）；cap3_fixed→cap4 **−157.1**（−157.8）；cap4→cap5 **−127.6**（−127.0）。其余 6 条在所报位数 **MATCH**。[斜率表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/slope.csv:8) · [生成器使用已舍入均值](/root/paper/foyer/scripts/lab/gen_slope.py:20) · [上游先舍入三位](/root/paper/foyer/scripts/lab/gen_arm_running.py:49) 正文相应的 −66.7、−157.8、−127.0 也需同步。[跨 trace 节](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_xtrace.tex:35) |
| host-tier 兑现范围、`idle_pooled.csv` 四种合并、`decomp.csv` 21 行空转分解及正文空转范围 | **MATCH**：静态 host-tier 为 90.2–92.4%，含控制臂为 84.2–92.4%；采样空转为 7.4–41.9%，长于 20 秒的段占空转时间 64.8–70.6%。[复用](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/reuse.csv:24) · [合并空转](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/idle_pooled.csv:6) · [分解](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/decomp.csv:6) |
| Table I 的 26 个 Δ | **MISMATCH 3 行**：running gate=4 为 **−25.39**（表 −25.38）；horizon=0 为 **−13.60**（−13.64）；horizon=2 为 **−4.81**（−4.84）。这三行的 `runs_precise.csv` 缺项，表值沿用了较粗输入。[表数据](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/table1.csv:19) · [原始日志之一](/root/paper/foyer/results/night/pois200_foyer_hz0/steps.jsonl:1) · [缺项时回退逻辑](/root/paper/foyer/scripts/lab/gen_table.py:124) |

Table I 另有 **5 行原始步骤日志在本地缺失**；其中 4 行可用 [`runs_precise.csv`](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/runs_precise.csv:1)核对，`default` 只有较粗的表输入。因此“其余 23 行一致”包含这项来源限制，不能称作 26 行全部独立原始日志复核。

## (3) 数值移动是否改变定性主张

**没有发现因这次数值移动而翻转的排序或判决。** 即便沿用 36.3% 下限、78.9% 中点及旧斜率，device-only 仍随并发明显下跌，host-tier 的 90.2–92.4% 静态范围仍与之分离；两条 trace 的斜率形状判断也不变。[模型表](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:243) · [跨 trace 判断](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_xtrace.tex:35)

用旧的一位小数前沿锚点与现有全精度锚点逐行比较，**Table I 无判决换档**；旧锚点使最接近边界的 horizon=1 约为 −0.944 pp，现为 −0.928 pp，均在 ±1 pp 平局带内。[锚点](/root/paper/foyer/scripts/lab/frontier.py:25) · [判决规则](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/table1.csv:4) 该点离 −1 pp 门槛仅约 **0.072 pp**，所以它本身较敏感；但**这次舍入修正**没有制造或消除平局。正文的“无人超过前沿”也不依赖上述几百分之一个百分点。[结果判词](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:10)

## Acceptance assessment

以目前稿件、仅凭本次三项数值审查，给 **约 20–35%** 的 CCF-B／CAS Q2 接收机会判断。定性负结果未靠舍入翻盘，是有利证据；但摘要级的 92.6% 错舍入、三条宣称使用全精度却实际用了三位均值的斜率，以及 Table I 三个回退到粗输入的 Δ，会削弱审稿人对整套测量流水线的信任。这个范围是审稿判断，不是打分或统计预测。
